# Skills · P · part 45

847 entries, perf to perf-trace. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **perf**: Measure a performance problem before touching it, find where the time goes, change one thing, re-measure, and report before and after numbers. Covers slow endpoints and queries, slow test suites and CI, and slow app screens backed by the backend. Also answers "does this change hurt performance" with numbers instead of opinion. Use for "it's slow", "why is this slow", "stays performant?", "does this cause perf issues", "measure and compare", "why does CI take 8m", or a perf issue number. Args: issue or PR number, endpoint, screen, test module, or a short description. ([mfakharealam/claude-code-engineering-skills](https://github.com/mfakharealam/claude-code-engineering-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/perf) · ★1)
- **perf**: Plan performance optimization. Explore bottlenecks, assess impact, then implement with optional spec creation. ([nullmastermind/godot-turnbase](https://github.com/nullmastermind/godot-turnbase/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/perf) · ★1 · 3 copies)

Use this skill whenever the user wants to understand why a program doesn't reach theoretical peak performance, needs combined top-down + perf hotspot analysis, or asks about multi-level bottleneck diagnosis. All perf-level analysis methods (record, annotate, SPE, PMU events, DDRC bandwidth) are covered by the perf-hotspot skill. ([kunpengcompute/skills](https://github.com/kunpengcompute/skills/tree/HEAD/BoostCoreSkills/skills/perf-topdown))
- **perf-torch-cuda-graphs**: Apply CUDA Graphs to PyTorch workloads — API selection (torch.compile, PyTorch make_graphed_callables, TE make_graphed_callables, MCore CudaGraphManager, FullCudaGraphWrapper, manual torch.cuda.graph), code compatibility, capture workflows, dynamic pattern handling, and troubleshooting. Triggers: CUDA graph, torch.cuda.graph, make_graphed_callables, reduce-overhead, graph capture, graph replay, kernel launch overhead, CudaGraphManager, FullCudaGraphWrapper, full-iteration graph, stream capture. ([NVIDIA/TensorRT-LLM](https://github.com/NVIDIA/TensorRT-LLM/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/perf-torch-cuda-graphs) · ★15k · 8 copies)
- **perf-torch-sync-free**: Identify and eliminate host-device synchronizations in PyTorch code. Detects sync points (.item(), .cpu(), boolean indexing, torch.tensor on CUDA), classifies false vs true dependencies, provides sync-free alternatives. Triggers: sync-free, synchronization, .item(), .cpu(), host-device sync, eliminate syncs, CPU stall, non_blocking, set_sync_debug_mode, cudaStreamSynchronize, cudaEventSynchronize, remove syncs, async GPU. ([NVIDIA/TensorRT-LLM](https://github.com/NVIDIA/TensorRT-LLM/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/perf-torch-sync-free) · ★15k · 9 copies)
- **perf-tp-dp-comm-overlap**: Operational guide for enabling TP, DP, and PP communication overlap in Megatron-Bridge, including config knobs, code anchors, pitfalls, and verification. ([MiseOsBrigade/MiseOS](https://github.com/MiseOsBrigade/MiseOS/tree/HEAD/docs/source-library/icloud/mise-enhanced-gpu-pack-a-punch-v0.2.0-repo-upgrade.zip/mise-enhanced-gpu-pack-a-punch-workflow-machine/vendor/nv-skills/skills/Megatron-Bridge/perf-tp-dp-comm-overlap) · 3 copies)
- **perf-trace**: Trace-driven throughput profiling for felis using `kitten __benchmark__` plus samply flamegraphs. Use when asked to measure or improve felis's parser/grid throughput (ascii, unicode, csi, long_escape_codes), benchmark felis against kitty, find a hot path in the daemon, or read a CPU profile of the parse loop. Covers driving the winit/Wayland app unattended, discarding the focus-warmup sample, decomposing cost with criterion micro-benches (pure dispatch vs decode vs full grid), capturing a samply profile of the daemon despite paranoid/mlock/attach/hand-off gotchas, and symbolicating raw addres… ([felis-terminal/felis](https://github.com/felis-terminal/felis/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/perf-trace) · ★9)
- **perf-trace**: Find where the time goes when something is slow by measuring it, then present a duration tree with each level's time and share. Use when the user says a page, job, request, or command is slow, laggy, or "taking so long", asks for "a stack trace with the duration of each level", or asks to check every page for slowness or missing data. Covers sampling profilers (py-spy, perf, samply, Node and Bun CPU profiles), OpenTelemetry traces, and browser page loads. Do not use for correctness bugs with no latency symptom. ([AnthonyAltieri/dotfiles](https://github.com/AnthonyAltieri/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/shared/perf-trace) · ★2)
- **perf-trace**: Capture a dotnet-trace / dotnet-counters session against the live backend and parse it with perftools (allocparse/cpuparse/dbparse) to attribute WHO is responsible for what L2 observed. Use when you know load behaves a certain way (L2) and need to find the responsible code — allocation churn, on-CPU time, or DB command shape. ([VirtoCommerce/ai-tools](https://github.com/VirtoCommerce/ai-tools/tree/HEAD/plugins/vc-perf/skills/perf-trace) · ★2 · 2 copies)
